(drive & steer rewards are always exponential, exp(-decay*|v|); only their weights vary. type = survival reward: 1/0 = binary, exp; definition of better/best: largest/er mean episode length achieved with shortest/er number of iterations, and evaluated through play_eval.py)

PHASE 2 (planned, gated on the Phase-1 grid) — ACTION-RATE PENALTY for chaos/jitter
  Prereq: DONE — `action_rate` term wired into BalanceRewardsCfg (weight 0 = off by
          default; func = balance_action_rate_penalty; returns -||a_t - a_{t-1}||_2,
          UNBOUNDED -> keep small), and --arp_w flag added to train.py /
          train_deterministic.py. Just pass --arp_w 0.1 (etc.) per run.
  Goal: does a SLIGHT smoothness penalty cut roll-RMS / action jitter WITHOUT
        shortening episodes? It penalizes action CHANGE, not motion -> smooths both
        motors, unlike steer_reward (which suppresses the steering the bike needs).
  Scope: apply only to the two LEADERS; arp=0 baselines already exist, so this is a
         controlled 1-var comparison. Do NOT cross arp with every grid cell.

    survival  drive  steer   arp    arp=0 baseline   note
    5 1/0      12     0.00    0.10   1826             smoothness on the steer=0 leader
    5 1/0      12     0.00    0.50   1826
    5 1/0     <winning drive/steer from Phase-1 grid>  0.10   <that run>   smoothness on the steer>0 leader
    5 1/0     <winning drive/steer from Phase-1 grid>  0.50   <that run>
  Read: arp should LOWER roll-RMS / action-jitter with ~unchanged episode length.
        If 0.50 helps at no cost, bracket up to 1.0; if it SHORTENS episodes it is
        too strong (over-damping the corrective control).

  spawn — drift_band and the drifted termination are blind to orbital motion, and a circle inside the
  band survives to timeout (would satisfy the >1400 criterion by circling). play_eval now reports
  "yaw mean/max" and "turn/episode" (360 deg = one full circle). Iter3 aborted ~20min in to fix the objective.

2026.9.8 After a little research on imu latency, major components are listed below from #1 ~ #11. As other terms are either negligible or can be fully eliminated by calibration, rl only focuses on #1: post-calibration initial bias (aka initial bias residual) and #8: white noise. Both the gyroscope and accelerometer have their #1 and #8 counterparts, in which the magnitude of #8 can be read from imu manual, but #1 have to be measured manually. Currently #8 is precisely set in code following imu datasheet, but #1 is only a reasonable prior placeholder (also noted in code comment). Thus, first find out how initial bias residual and white noise of imu affect the policy. #8 should take effect every run onwards, while #1 is still an adjustable dr term.

2026.9.11 Attempted to transfer 09090950 model 500 (mean epi len 1500) to mujoco for sim-to-sim verification. However, discovered large gap between isaaclab and mujoco simulation environments. First, the minimum physical step dt is different between them. However, the upstream policy inference frequency are aligned to 50Hz. Second, collision meshes are implemented differently. In isaaclab, due to a lack of pre-defined collision configuration in usda file, convex hulls were employed to handle collision. However, mujoco only supports primitive collision shapes (cuboids, spheres, cylinders......). Third, groun/wheel friction, motor dynamics, bike inertia, initial condition, observation consistency remain largely unexplored.

2026.9.12 So the next step is: First, verify mujoco and isaaclab simulation environment consistency (simulator metrics). Third, verify evaluation metric consistency based on the Tsinghua bike model.

2026.10.1 Discovered one difference between deploy/ and Trackstand-Balance-v0 on policy raw output handling: Trackstand-Balance-v0 doesn't impose any limit or clipping on raw output. It only multiplies 1.57 and 6.28 respectively to correspond to the control method of the motor. However, deploy/ caps raw policy output between -1 to 1, then performs scaling. It is proven that even without any limit, the raw policy outputs rarely exceed motor's position and torque limit. Thus, removed the limit on raw policy output of deploy/. Exported .onnx policy of model_91850.pt from 09221945, and do deployment. Try to draw graphs of 1. Raw policy output. 2. Scaled policy output. 3. Real-time motor torque response.

2026.10.1 Carried out three test stages A, B, and C as stated in BIKE_DIAGNOSTIC_TESTS.md under deploy/docs/. They try to find the cause of the failed deployment from three submodules of the system: sensor bias (A), policy raw output (B), and motor response (C). Recorded respective rosbags, and analyzed their data. Conclusion: The steering motor performs unstable target tracking. It reacts with large oscillation even under a constant position command. Currently, policy outputs position target for steering motor at 50Hz. Then, a 200Hz explicit PD loop is employed to convert position target to Q-axis current (torque/torque_constant) target. Then the current target is sent to steering motor at 200Hz.

2026.10.2 ABC stage test analysis report in deploy/reports/rosbag_ABC_2026-10-02.

2026.10.3 Under 10Hz +-2 rad sine wave command, steering motor follows with ~26ms latency, but it didn't achieve 2 rad peak. Instead, only 0.5 rad. Under 1Hz, however, it achieves 2 rad peak. Does high command frequency exceed the motor's ability to follow position?

2026.10.4 Tested steering motor response of C2 command under a 2/5/10Hz sine wave of amplitudes 0.1/0.5/1/2 rad (amplitude is the half of peak to peak). Report see deploy/experiments/reports/. Conclusion: phase lag worsen as frequency of sine wave (position command is sent at 200Hz fixed, but the sine wave itself has different periods/frequencies) increase. And the attenuation also worsen as frequency of sine wave increases (motor peaks at lower percentages of sine amplitudes). Basically, C2 commands could not achieve on-time and full-scale position command following. Investigating into MIT control protocol.

2026.10.5 Implemented sine wave (let the steering motor position follow a sine wave) test using mit control -> steering_sine_mit.py, and its corresponding motor state collector motor_feedback_mit.py, all under deploy/experiments/. Now testing the mit control performance. There are multiple free variables to test: Kp, Kd, sine wave frequency, sine wave amplitude. First take Kp and Kd = 10 and 0.2. Do the similar tests of 2/5/10Hz with 0.1/0.5/1/2 rad variations. See if mit performs better than C2 control and effectively alleviates the vibration problem discovered in C3 control.

Only missing results, later corrections and subsequent progress are summarized below. The original notes above are preserved. Dates refer to the recorded work, not new experiments on 2026.10.12. Detailed methods, graphs, exact metrics and provenance remain in ../../../progress_record.md and its linked reports. Later corrections supersede conflicting early interpretations.

2026.8.24-27 Earlier training/debugging findings: binary survival 5, drive reward about 12, steer reward 0, entropy 0.005 and small [32, 32] networks gave the first working nominal balance. The intended 50 Hz was actually 30 Hz because assigning cfg.step_dt was ineffective; setting sim.dt=0.01 fixed it. Compare survival seconds or timeout rate, not episode steps across rates. A/B best episode lengths were 36 (entropy 0.003/30 Hz), 375 (0.005/30 Hz), and 1417 (0.005/50 Hz).

2026.8.26-27 Laptop/desktop difference was partly different Isaac Lab physics, not just GPU/seed: deprecated velocity_limit was ignored on the newer desktop version, allowing rear speed 2420 rad/s instead of 61.6. Unbounded observations drove critic loss to NaN, then actor/std to NaN. Use velocity_limit_sim and check effective limits; even simulator speed caps can be soft. Historical uncapped torque results require remeasurement. Inspect value loss and observation magnitudes before changing entropy/LR.

2026.8.28-9.7 Full DR and warm-starting a nominal specialist both failed; bigger [512,256,128, 64] networks did not solve it. Later term-isolation showed friction/mass can accelerate learning, COM-y and gyro bias are the main harmful terms, while COM-x/z and tilt bias are tolerable. Do not interpret the old full-DR failures as proof that every DR term is harmful.

2026.9.3-7 Missing corrected play_eval results are listed below. Units: episode length in policy steps, timeout in %, roll RMS in degrees. RAW/SM are TensorBoard-selected raw/smoothed checkpoints, not necessarily final models. Old nominal-only evaluations of DR policies and the 50.6-step drift-Gaussian evaluation were wrong-regime comparisons; only corrected results should rank policies.

2026.9.3-7 Corrections to reward verdicts: wheel-net band 1.0 at model_44200 reduced wheel deaths to 8.4%, with drift 22.9%; late finals hid its benefit. Drift quadratic's 1413.8 steps did not make it uniformly better: roll RMS 1.21 versus band 0.46. COM-y tolerance lies between +/-0.005 and +/-0.01 m; all-axis COM used x/y +/-0.02 m and z +/-0.05 m, not x+z only. The per-axis z test used +/-0.02 m; z +/-0.05 m alone and combined x+z were not tested. G 2 and drift-only IMU/COM campaigns differ in rewards, so cross-campaign comparisons are confounded.

2026.9.8-9 Observation/noise correction: white observation noise was disabled in all 71 inspected pre -9.8 balance-run env.yaml files despite authored std values. Per-step white noise and constant episode residual bias are separate mechanisms. Dropping the four joint channels was the cause of the later 9-dim failure, not datasheet-level white noise alone. Frozen-policy channel ablation measures reliance, not whether those channels can be removed during training.

2026.9.9 09090950 model_500 had max roll 3.00 deg and turn 58.9 deg/episode; model_32400 had max roll 0.87 deg and turn 21.7 deg/episode. Both had zero deaths; 500 was chosen for tighter average roll. Final 59999 was not evaluated in this record. The 13-dim/noise-off comparison was 1401 steps/70.5% timeout; 9-dim/noise-off's manual 438.2 and TB peak 911 are different reported snapshots, not interchangeable evaluation results.

2026.9.8-9 IMU model settled on per-step gravity std 0.001 g and gyro std 0.0012 rad/s, with zero noise on remaining channels. Episode residuals need measurement after the actual firmware calibration, over 10-30 cold boots; a single fixed bias is not their randomization std. Use rad/s, not deg/s. Measure filtered white noise, packet latency and Allan deviation from a 1-3 h stationary log. The authored 0.03 rad/s gyro bias is not automatically a realistic calibrated residual. No such hardware characterization was completed by the noise-model work.

2026.9.13-17 Simulator corrections: enable_external_forces_every_iteration=False was a major cause of old NewBike rear-effort underdelivery; True restores rigid response even at solver 4/2. Trackstand used True and did not need the old x 24.5 inertia compensation: an in-air 0.5 Nm probe delivered 97-100% of theoretical response. The later gap was contact/steering-roll coupling, not mismatched mass or drive underdelivery.

2026.9.14-17 SDF/collision details: approximation="sdf" alone falls back to hull; Physx SDF Mesh Collision API plus the token is required. sdf Margin is relative, not a 1 cm contact cradle. Hull also showed the bounded-lean artifact, so blaming SDF alone was retracted: PhysX single-contact resolution was implicated. Wheel-slice success claims were invalidated by malformed USD arrays running without wheels; slicing was reverted. Cylinder contact then removed the bounded equilibrium and required retraining. Physical models were subsequently verified; these are historical diagnosis corrections, not new model changes.

2026.9.16-17 Trackstand task parity: steering position action scale 1.57 rad, rear effort scale 6.28 Nm, 13 observations/2 actions; steering implicit gains 10/0.2 with 5.6 Nm and 23.9 rad/s limits, rear 0/0.05 with 6.28 Nm and 61.6 rad/s. Front wheel is unactuated, so "2 != 3 actuators" is expected. Terminations: 30 s timeout, 30 deg fall, diverged limits 20/50/300, drift 0.5 m, yaw 30 deg, net-wheel angle 7.2 rad. Copy the complete MDP package; partial imports of dynamic terms can fail.

2026.9.17 09161648 ended at 158600 iterations. TB raw/smoothed peaks selected model_15650; eval 64 envs x 3000 steps gave both model_15650 and final 158600 100% timeout, but roll RMS 0.23 versus 0.68 deg and much calmer steering favored 15650. Its cylinder-contact transfer was marginal, so high in-domain success did not establish cross-contact robustness.

2026.9.18 09171946 completed at 224199 iterations. Raw TB latest tied peak selected model_99050; EMA -0.6 selected model_41050. In 64-env x 3000-step evaluations, 41050 had 128/128 full-length episodes, roll RMS 0.86 deg, drift max 0.005 m, yaw max 1.58 deg; 99050 had 95.3% timeouts and 4.7% yaw deaths. Thus 41050 remained the deployment candidate. Its ONNX agreement was 1.19e-7. The early 9000-step/180 s zero-termination MuJoCo run was seed-specific: seed 42 later showed two pre-existing early falls. Do not read that milestone as guaranteed zero falls across seeds.

2026.9.18 MuJoCo observation correction: free-joint qvel[3: 6] is body-frame angular velocity; linear velocity uses R^T*qvel[0: 3]. The previous velocimeter path applied a second rotation. Removed unnecessary IMU-site/sensors from the deployment augmentation and used direct state access. Seed 42 A/B gave 2 falls in both versions, roll RMS 1.13/1.11 deg; the fix did not create the existing transient.

2026.9.18-22 Ground spawn was calibrated to base z=-0.016 m in Isaac and -0.0156 m in MuJoCo. Physics rates and policy rates are separate: 41050 trained at 100 Hz physics/50 Hz policy; current Trackstand uses 400 Hz/50 Hz (dt=0.0025, decimation 8). MuJoCo stays at 400 Hz, as its 100 Hz integration trials were unstable even though Isaac 100 Hz works. render_interval=decimation avoids eight GUI renders per policy step.

2026.9.22 Matched 100/400 Hz Isaac training comparison: at model_123850, timeouts 50.3/51.7%, fallen 0.7/4.0%, yaw deaths 46.9/43.0%, roll RMS 1.01/1.40 deg. No systematic learning-quality advantage was measured; 400 Hz cost 51.6 versus 29.2 ms/env-step, about 1.77 x. Recorded 09190125 final 499999 reached 75.4% timeout. These evaluations supplement, rather than replace, the old manual xxxx-at 7850 placeholder.

2026.9.21-22 MuJoCo ported all six DR reset terms, independently per environment/episode, with nominal restoration before each reset (no compounding): per-body mass/inertia U(0.8, 1.2), shared per-bike COM offset +/-0.005 m, collider friction buckets, actuator gains U(0.9, 1.1), initial roll +/-0.10 rad/rate +/-0.25 rad/s, gyro/gravity residuals. --dr enables it; default is no DR. White observation noise is separate. The no-DR 41050 policy also failed under full DR in Isaac; MuJoCo DR failures alone did not imply a porting bug.

2026.9.22 Friction semantics corrected: PhysX's old average(ground 1, tire U(0.8, 1.2)) produced effective U(0.9, 1.1), outside nominal 0.75. Switched tire/default material to min-combine, then retargeted nominal relative friction to 0.65 and DR to 0.5-0.8. MuJoCo uses floor 0.1 and explicit tire coefficients so MAX gives the same pair. Exported per-geom friction and solref had overridden default-class edits; both were fixed explicitly. solref 0.005/1.5 revalidated 41050 with zero falls in the recorded checks. Mass parity was about 2.4243 kg. Older DR runs retained their old friction regime.

2026.9.22-23 Simplified Trackstand to one TrackstandRLEnv/TrackstandRLEnvCfg and TRACKSTAND_ROBOT_CFG, preserving active rewards (survival 5, drift Gaussian 2 sigma 0.25, rate penalty 0.1). A 37-check headless parity audit passed. Removed CycleRL, fixed the noise function's stale module string and training/eval registration imports; some old CycleRL-only helper/SKRL paths remained unported. Directory/Git reorganization preserved live training and relative USD payloads. MuJoCo model names became trackstand_rl_0916_bike and trackstand_rl_0914_bike; root Git excludes large generated/vendor/log assets. These were reorganizations, not new physics experiments.

2026.9.23-25 Deployment implementation details missing above: stationary balancing uses only IMU quaternion/rates and steering/rear encoders, with longitudinal velocity estimated as 0.07*rear_speed and lateral velocity zero. RC sticks do not control mobility; CH 7 high/low/middle means stands up/down/hold with eight-frame debounce. Receiver is CRSF/ELRS on CP 2102 at 420000 baud (not the obsolete SBUS interpretation), IMU CH 340 at 100 Hz, servo UART /dev/ttyAMA 2; servo IDs 1/2 up 1648/2454, down 1972/2085. IMU/vendor and servo libraries are packaged under YbImuLib/ and servo_control/. The later recorded IMU roll correction is 1.42 deg.

2026.9.24-25 IMU audit caveat: packet-global freshness stops completely frozen streams, but split quaternion/gyro packets can still mix ages and torn caches. Persistent 6/9-axis fusion mode, filter delay and dynamic acceleration/magnetic error were not measured. Vendor payload-length/endian/RX-thread/reconnect and per-field coherence checks remained gaps. Proposed fixes were per-type timestamps/counters, locked snapshots, exact length checks, diagnostics/reconnect and explicitly verified fusion mode; these were recommendations, not completed implementation.

2026.9.27 Isaac DR made opt-in: train.py, train_deterministic.py, play.py and play_eval.py now default to nominal and accept --dr; removed --no_dr. White noise remains on without DR. --dr restores full reset events without a ramp: mass 0.8-1.2, COM+/-5 mm, 32 friction buckets 0.5-0.8, stiffness/damping 0.9-1.1, roll+/-0.10 rad, roll-rate+/-0.25 rad/s, gyro residual std 0.0002 rad/s and gravity residual 0.002 g. Ten tests and real 16-env x 96-step nominal/DR smoke checks passed. Reproduce old full-DR evaluations with --dr; the short smoke was not a benchmark.

2026.9.27-10.1 Verified motor direction convention: native positive C0 turns steering left/rear backward; native positive A2/A3 feedback is steering left/rear forward. Deployment signs are steering encoder/current -1/-1, rear +1/-1, gear 1; steering TX/RX 102/002, rear 101/001. Positive policy steering/feedback is right, rear is forward. The older ros2_ws steering +1/rear -1 encoder assumptions were superseded. A2 int32[1: 5] uses 0.01 rpm; A3 multiturn int32[3: 7] uses 16384 counts/rev; B0 float[2: 6] is Nm/A; A1/C0 int32[1: 5] is 0.001 A. Policy receives signed startup-relative wrapped position; net rotation requires accumulation rather than a single wrapped sample.

2026.10.1-2 ABC details: all three stages used 41050, not 91850. A was actually dry_run=false but outputs stayed disabled; IMU roll-rate correlation 0.978 and steering/rear encoder consistency 0.991/0.992 showed no gross sign/unit error. A had 953 missing records, one corrupted IMU payload and a roughly 0.8 s gap, excluded from comparisons. B was dry_run=true: 1718 real ONNX actions replayed within 4.6566e-8, scaling matched exactly; policy period median 19.98 ms, inference median 0.289 ms. It did not drive the motors or test dynamic recovery. An apparent 338.5 ms bag gap was transport/recording delay, not a control stall.

2026.10.2 C used fixed commands with the policy absent and free steering/rear wheel. A -0.02 rad steering target produced roughly 14.25 Hz oscillation over -18.918 to +19.556 deg; consecutive steering torque changes reached 8.72 Nm. Query failures and feedback ages up to 26.23 ms were measured. The explicit 10*error -0.2*speed law was reconstructed exactly; unstable low-level steering is established without policy variation. The earlier text's "C3 vibration" is misleading: this fixed-target test used host PD plus C0, and no measured C3 tracking baseline was identified. Rear +/-0.05 Nm tests delivered current but tiny displacement had opposite expected signs; the coupled test did not establish the cause, so do not blindly flip signs.

2026.10.2 Four external torque updates per 50 Hz target are not proven sufficient or insufficient by frequency alone. First changed steering feedback appeared about 25 ms after a small step; 90% crossing about 47 ms, with persistent bias. Another re-enable held one target 1.824 s without settling and showed near-zero current for about 150 ms despite requested torque. This supports delay/enable/control-path investigation, not a universal firmware time constant. C2/C3/MIT internal position/current-loop rates are undocumented. Proposed StageD rate/step/re-enable tests and tighter guarded steering-only mode were not completed.

2026.10.2-3 Continuous ROS sine helper uses one persistent 50 Hz publisher; a literal ros2 topic pub with fixed YAML does not generate varying samples. The 1002110109 sine bag was original C0/host-PD, not C2. Retained combined command/position/torque graph and four separate command/position/speed/torque plots without smoothing or artificial shifts; source bag unchanged.

2026.10.2-3 Replaced the guarded C3 prototype with C2 in motor_io_position.py. C3's repeated relative reference was not established, so target-minus-measured-position was unsafe to assume. C2 sends startup_A3_zero + round(sign*gear*target_rad*16384/(2*pi)), little-endian int32, with no measured-position subtraction or accumulation. Zero survives RC cycles; restart after motor origin/reset. Before powered C2, B2 speed and B3 current caps are written/read back, rounded down to 23.9 rad/s and 5.6 Nm. Host steering PD is absent; firmware PI gains are not assumed equal to 10/0.2. The raw C2 sine helper lacks those deployment guards and disable-on-exit.

2026.10.3-4 C2 quantitative correction: 12 free-steering conditions (2/5/10 Hz x amplitude 0.1/0.5/1/2 rad), command transmission about 197 Hz. At +/-0.1 rad, gain was 63.5/32.5/16.9%; at +/-1 rad, 66.2/33.7/17.9%, with phase-equivalent lags 67.51/39.42/22.55 ms. Even small demands below 23.9 rad/s attenuated, so high-speed saturation alone cannot explain it. Descriptive fit H=K/(1+j*w*tau) gave K 1.0053, tau 90.35 ms, inferred corner 1.76 Hz, not a measured cutoff or pure delay. Final fixed-target median error was within one encoder count. The old roughly 26 ms/~0.5 rad peak statement is not a universal delay/full-wave amplitude: the 10 Hz +/-2 rad steady fundamental was 0.3543 rad with 22.65 ms phase-equivalent lag. Graph/HTML/report bundles are in deploy/experiments/reports/c2_sine_sweep_20261004/. Missing-data gaps are shaded rather than interpolated.

2026.10.4 Tsinghua controller comparison recorded in tsinghua_bike_project/lqr_pid_control_process.html: LQR uses [steering angle, roll, roll-rate] and commands C1 steering speed; the PID cascade commands C0 current. They are not interchangeable with RL angle/rear-torque actions. Nominal LQR is 200 Hz with 50 Hz gain refresh and speed scheduled at minimum 1 m/s; it is not a validated zero-speed trackstand model. PID's steering subscriber incorrectly reads a published timestamp as velocity; optional gain scheduling also has stale-matrix/Qc-Rc defects. These were documented, not repaired.

2026.10.4-5 MIT protocol review: an 8-byte high-bit-first frame on 0x502 contains position 16 bits, velocity 12, Kp 12, Kd 12 and torque 12, with no opcode. Firmware law is Kp*(p_target-p)+Kd*(v_target-v)+feedforward torque. Position/velocity/torque use symmetric ranges, gains use 0-500 and 0-5 non negative mappings. The supplied vendor helper incorrectly encodes gains symmetrically: requested zero becomes about Kp 250/Kd 2.5; it was not used for correct deployment packing. C0 is current, C1 speed, C2 absolute position, C3 relative position, all opcode+int32/DLC 5. MIT naming refers to the MIT robotics control style, not identical firmware across manufacturers.

2026.10.5 MIT experiment sender was confirmed functional on the bike. It verifies CF, B1, F0, A3, F1, uses absolute native centre plus signed sine offset, and writes persistent origin/Pos_Max3rad while retaining actual velocity/torque ranges. Requested zero velocity/feedforward encodes to small residuals +0.010989 rad/s/+0.004396 Nm; Kp 10/Kd 0.2 decode to 10.012210/0.200244. F0 values are encoding ranges, not verified total-PD torque limits. motor_feedback_mit.py automatically writes CSV and only queries/observes; it does not enable, disable or reset a motor. Strict audits covered every opcode/length and reference/range epochs.

2026.10.5-6 Integrated steering_sine_mit_io.py combines steering-only 50 Hz MIT commands and nominal 200 Hz extended feedback with automatic CSV, one CAN owner, asynchronous queries and a bounded logger. Rear collection is read-only. Missing F1, mode/fault/origin/range changes, interference, overflow or logger failure stop it; CF shutdown confirmation is attempted. Its default period is 1 s, not the old 10 s doc/test assumption; the mismatch was corrected without changing waveform defaults. The original standalone sender remains 200 Hz/10 s. Both are standard-library standalone tools, not ROS deployment nodes.

2026.10.5-6 Added motor_mit_io.py for MIT steering+C0 rear, preserving policy angle/rear-torque semantics. Initial rates were steering 50 Hz, rear/safety 200 Hz; later 50/100/200 division supersedes them below. Startup writes B1 once, verifies F0/A3/F1/B0, and RC cycles retain zero. Native gains 10/0.2, requested velocity/feedforward 0, +/-90 deg target, 23.9 rad/s slew, rear damping 0.05 and 6.28 Nm clamps remain. This removes the host delayed steering-PD calculation, but does not prove loaded tracking or balance. Rear MIT direction is unverified; retained C0 is the sign-tested baseline.

2026.10.5 MIT safety audit fixed ignoring rear AE/CF faults, stale queued feedback timestamps, unsolicited startup-zero capture, unchecked disable acknowledgements and oversized re-enable slew. One kernel-timestamped SocketCAN owner rejects overflow/backlog/clock uncertainty and matches query deadlines. Either motor hardware fault stops both and hard-latches. CF/zero-CF disable waits for both replies up to 250 ms, retries failed sends every 20 ms and blocks new motion pending confirmation. Send success is not execution/free-state proof. Recorded applicable software suite 289 passed/1 skipped; no bike timing was certified.

2026.10.6 Deployment refactor removed 493 runtime lines; total Python tree 14184->13832. C2 888->465 lines reused the base; MIT 940->879 initially did too. Tests then 352 deployment/117 experiment/5 analysis passed (1 socket skip), with 3000 matching mock ticks. At user request MIT was subsequently separated: directly inherits ROS Node, owns state/interfaces, imports neither motor_io/motor_io_position nor python-can, but shares pure helpers. Size 879->1031; 386 deployment tests passed/1 skipped. Most additional lines handle transport/preflight/fault/disable confirmations, not an external PD loop. Counts are dated snapshots, not performance measurements.

2026.10.6 Unified all three motor backends on motor_state_v2: [steering_pos, rear_pos, steering_vel, rear_vel, Unix_publish_time, steering_torque, rear_torque]. Removed separate motor_torque topic; policy still reads only first 4. Torque is signed measured A1/C0 current times B0, not commanded or calibrated shaft torque; stale/missing torque is NaN, asynchronously cached and unclipped. F1 torque stays in motor_control. The then-current full suite 439 passed/1 skipped. MIT publishes five topics (state, control, event, fault, status) and subscribes only motor_command; it does not record CSV/rosbags automatically. Use a separate ros2 bag record process.

2026.10.6 Legacy gaps remain: C0/C2 do not await CF confirmations or implement MIT AE/CF monitoring; centred disabled steering can clear an over speed latch before actual speed/freshness recovers. Servo acknowledgements do not prove physical support and status error bytes were not fully enforced. IMU per-field coherence and legacy double-shutdown issues remain. Fixing these was outside the behavior-preserving refactor. Relocated historical analyzers may require explicit repository/bag paths. Branch main was observed in that dated check; no rename author was inferred.

2026.10.7 Rear action law audit: training uses 6.28*raw_action-0.05*rear_speed; deployment explicitly uses clip(clip(request, +/-6.28)-0.05*speed, +/-6.28), then torque_sign*torque/B0 for C0. MIT with Kp=Kd=0 and raw feedforward would omit trained damping. At Kt 0.30, C0 resolution is 0.0003 Nm; MIT +/-18 Nm 12-bit encoding is 0.008791 Nm (~29 x coarser) and cannot represent exact zero. Rear F0 range/current ramp was not measured. No demonstrated rear torque-only MIT advantage justified changing the C0 baseline.

2026.10.1 audit rechecked on 2026.10.7: saved model_91850 evaluation was 64 envs x 3000 policy steps, seed 42, full DR. Rear requests exceeded 6.28 Nm on 171/192000 samples (0.0891%), all at episode starts, reaching 132.002426 Nm; afterward max 2.76509 Nm. PhysX received the unbounded effort buffer, while clipped diagnostic torque peaked 6.28 Nm, so a clipped torque plot does not establish a hardware-equivalent total torque cap. This was saved-data analysis, not another evaluation. Artifact: isaaclab_train/reports/motor_limits_09221945_91850_2026-10-01/.

Motor state still required new/fresh paired replies, not exactly 200 Hz publication. Reducing it to 50 Hz would add observation age and couple to 60 ms policy motor timeout; merely reducing poll_rate also slows polling. No separate 50 Hz state reduction was implemented. Later MIT profile uses 100 Hz state and 200 Hz control diagnostics.

2026.10.8 Implemented MIT deployment frequency profile: 50 Hz policy input (disable callbacks unthrottled), 100 Hz A3/A2/A1 query rounds per motor plus state/status/fault, 200 Hz steering MIT/rear C0/safety/control telemetry. Six serialized 2 ms query timeouts can exceed a 10 ms round; missed slots are skipped, no catch-up bursts. State is up to 100 Hz because both pairs must be new/fresh; automatic F1/C0 replies still arrive continuously. F0 checks/missing B0 retries remain 1 s. Initial software validation 486 deployment tests passed/1 skipped.

2026.10.8 Urgent MIT audit fixes: active command 80 ms or encoder/B0 readiness 50 ms failures now latch instead of auto-resuming after transient recovery; each motor send rechecks after encoding/lock waits with the original command timestamp. Disable-generation tracking prevents rapid down/up from cancelling an active stop. A failed steering send withholds nonzero rear C0. All 21 MIT parameters are startup-only/read-only; runtime dry_run changes are rejected, not emergency stops. Separate logical slew timing fixes dry-run jitter, first 200 Hz step 0.1195 rad and delayed cap 0.239 rad. Terminal stop events follow CAN cleanup while ROS remains valid. Refactor 1069->1066 lines; 515 deployment tests/1 skipped plus 130 experiment tests and 1000 healthy mock ticks passed for that dated snapshot, not later versions.

2026.10.8-9 CF/F1 confirmation changed: fault-free CF mode 0 confirms directly; retained mode 1-5 needs a subsequent fresh F1 status 0 (MIT inactive/fault-free). Unknown/faulted/active/stale/missing replies fail closed. The saved guide reports a live CF/F1 plus rear-zero probe, no B1/F0 writes/MIT motion, with both confirmations; this is attributed recorded evidence, not a new balancing validation. Inactive MIT plus CF reply is not an independent power-stage/free-state measurement. CAN-UP preflight and descriptive interface/ID/payload/errno errors were added; after ROS shutdown cleanup still runs but no terminal ROS event is promised.

2026.10.9 Safety snapshot: policy_controller removed the 30 deg roll cutoff and max_roll_rad declaration/read, retaining gravity_body[2]>0 inversion detection, freshness, motor faults and inference checks. The leftover YAML max_roll_rad has no effect. MIT removed measured steering over travel/over speed stops; legacy C0/C2 still retain +/-90 deg measured-angle and 26.29/67.76 rad/s speed checks. Target+/-90 deg/slew 23.9 rad/s/rear 6.28 Nm bounds remain. Ordinary MIT latch clearing requires enable off, confirmed disable, fresh encoder/B0 readiness, no pending/active output and unwrapped steering strictly inside+/-5 deg; hard faults require restart. Policy faults do not automatically lower kickstands. RC down requests motor disable and stands down without waiting for CAN acknowledgements.

2026.10.9 Validation/docs snapshot: seven deployment guides/audits were synchronized and historical passing counts dated. Experiment suite 130 passed. These failures were documented, not repaired by the recording/docs tasks; no later full-pass, loaded tracking or successful policy balance is claimed here.

2026.10.9 Pi "No executable found" occurs before Python/CAN initialization when the selected installation lacks a matching executable wrapper. setup.py already registers motor_mit_io; synchronize the whole package, colcon build --symlink-install --packages-select trackstand_deploy, source install/setup.bash, then inspect ros2 pkg prefix/executables. Sourcing alone does not create wrappers. The Pi's unique cause/completed fix was not remotely verified.

2026.10.9 Remaining priorities: establish real firmware MIT total-current/torque protection and motor-side CAN deadman (CD live setting unknown, protocol default disabled/non persistent 5000 ms); active AE is consumed, not queried 100 Hz, so a 100 Hz ROS fault topic is not 100 Hz hardware fault sampling. Verify physical disabling, rear sign/low-torque response, coherent IMU calibration/freshness, matched 50 Hz-held/200 Hz-dispatch tracking and independently supported loaded steering. Then model measured delays/limits/friction/noise in simulation and judge policy transfer. Do not blame/replace policy or bike solely from unloaded sine tests or software pass counts.

2026.10.13 Complete PRE-DISABLE command flow (snapshot c4b2df7d63bf978ff784f909f872c7545e10ea58, recorded 2026.10.14):
IMU + signed motor_state first 4 -> policy_controller builds 13 observations -> OnnxPolicy.act -> raw[a_s, a_d].
/trackstand/raw_action publishes original values; scale_policy_action steer=clip(1.57*a_s, +/-pi/2), rear=6.28*a_d.
/trackstand/motor_command=[enable, steer_rad, rear_Nm] -> motor_mit_io._command_callback.
Callback keeps requested_* diagnostics but stores steer=clip(request, +/-steering_limit), rear=clip(request, +/-6.28), command timestamp/disable generation under lock.
200 Hz _control_tick snapshots BOUNDED stored targets, not unbounded requested fields; freshness/fault gate rejects unsafe/missing-data state and uses confirmed disable.
Steer when due: step_max=23.9*min(elapsed_since_logical_send, 0.010), first elapsed 0.005; next=previous+clip(target-previous, +/-step_max).
Steer native=MIT_zero+encoder_sign*gear*next -> absolute MIT packing/travel-bounded quantization -> recheck guard -> CAN 0x502 -> motor internal PD.
Rear damping=-0.05*reported_speed; final=clip(stored_rear+damping, +/-6.28).
Rear native current=torque_sign*final/B0_Kt -> signed int32 LE milliamps C0 -> recheck guard -> CAN 0x101 -> motor current control.
motor_control reports requested/bounded/slewed/encoded/send flags; A3/A2/A1/C0/F1 feedback -> signed state/current telemetry -> next policy observation.
RC down/faults withhold motion and disable; neither sender restores raw requests downstream. 50 Hz policy holds targets between 200 Hz dispatches, not four inferences/frame.

2026.10.16 Reduced FOC model explained, not identified: Lq*diq/dt=vq-Rs*iq-Ke*omega; tau_e=Kt*iq; J*domega/dt=tau_e-b*omega-tau_load; dtheta/dt=omega(approximately id 0). Consistent rotor/output coordinates required, phase-to-phase electrical specs not blindly dq parameters. C0 protocol p 13 is 0.001 A signed Q current, current*Kt torque; p 23 current-loop diagram does not prescribe outer -0.05*speed. Real passive losses still exist. First-order lag/dead time needs identification; sine phase lag is not universal transport delay.

2026.10.16 PhysX obtains inertia from USD masses, COM, tensors/principal axes, joint transforms, coupled dynamics, not ImplicitActuator torque/speed ratings. Active rear mass 0.12332977 kg, principal inertia 0.00017797352/0.000178/0.0003290265 kg m2; one component is not automatically total effective J. armature adds unrepresented joint inertia; ideal gear reflection N^2*J rotor, N=rotor/joint speed, avoid double count. Unset armature/friction inherits defaults, not proof zero inertia/losses. Verified model geometry unchanged.

2026.10.16 Actuator choice qualified: Implicit is valid both motors; Kp=Kd=0 still permits feedforward torque. Steering implicit PD reasonable MIT baseline, not proven best. Explicit IdealPD makes software PD/torque clipping explicit; DCMotor adds linear 4 quadrant torque-speed envelope, not full electrical model; delayed/learned response needs data. Class swap alone does not fit sampled damping/current-loop/bandwidth/loaded response. No experiment proves alternative class superior; preserve old checkpoint dynamics, evaluate/retrain revisions separately.

2026.10.16 IMPORTANT CORRECTION to Markdown 44/text 2026.10.7: ImplicitActuator.damping is controller Kd, NOT passive motor b. tau_request=Kp*(target-q)+Kd*(target speed-speed)+tau_ff; plant J*omega_dot=tau_actual-b*speed-load. Kd and b both Nms/rad, but physical roles differ. Zero-target speed unsaturated algebra looks alike; clip(controller)-b*speed differs from clip(controller-b*speed). Current rear damping 0.05 comment says bearing friction, so documented intent conflates plant loss/controller gain. Conceptually pure torque uses Kp 0/Kd 0, viscous_friction=b_measured; 0.05 is NOT experimentally identified b. No implementation change made.

2026.10.16 Physical losses should NOT automatically be copied as Pi -0.05*speed: real mechanism already has losses, host term adds virtual braking/can double count. If intentionally designed feedback braking, model/implement it explicitly while accounting separate physical losses. Illustrative real b 0.05+host Kd 0.05 gives 0.10 total in simple unsaturated model, NOT bike measurement. Existing Pi damping 0.05 remains active in all backends. Steering MIT Kd 0.2 stays controller damping, independent passive b belongs viscous_friction; do not arbitrarily increase Kd. Fit static/Coulomb/viscous losses under controlled current/speed/load tests; moving coefficients/removing host drag changes dynamics/saturation and may require policy retraining. No on-bike A/B or remedy validated.

2026.10.16 Friction mapping(modern PhysX): friction=static/breakaway effort tau_s, dynamic_friction=moving Coulomb tau_c, viscous_friction=b. Moving J*omega_dot=tau_e-b*omega-tau_c*sign(omega)-load. At rest static friction balances net effort up totau_s, not always added to dynamic friction; solver uses impulses. Modern angular static/dynamic units Nm, b Nms/rad at SI interface; IsaacSim 4.5 static was dimensionless/load-dependent, installed docs say effort for 5.0+. Raw USD angular coefficient may be per degree, convert correct API units. These are joint losses, not tire-ground friction or MIT Kd.

2026.10.16 Full installed ImplicitActuatorCfg has 12 inherited/own fields: class_type, joint_names_expr, effort_limit, velocity_limit, effort_limit_sim, velocity_limit_sim, stiffness, damping, armature, friction, dynamic_friction, viscous_friction. class_type implicit default; provide joint selection/gains, gains may None to inherit. Numeric fields scalar/joint-regex dict; optional None means inherited/resolved, not zero. Prefer_sim limits; effort_limit alias, velocity_limit alone ignored for implicit, inconsistent paired aliases error. Travel, mass/tensors, action scales/rates, electrical R/L/Kt/Ke, runtime targets/constructor joint IDs/device/env count are not extra user actuator-config fields. New online branches differ; installed code authoritative.

2026.10.16 Implicit warning clarified: the broad should-not-be-used wording is misleading, not a ban on ImplicitActuatorCfg. Articulation installs gains in PhysX/forwards targets; solver executes PD. Python compute returns original command, calculates approximate diagnostic effort, not final torque command. Do not use it as standalone software target-to-torque controller. IdealPD instead replaces actions with calculated/clipped torque, clears pos/vel, and PhysX gains are zeroed to avoid double PD. Official examples use implicit cfg; new develop docs omit blanket caution. No inference of undocumented author intent; current gains are effective, no manual USD edit required.

2026.10.16 User's lumping argument is mathematically CORRECT under ideal unsaturated zero-target speed assumptions: Kp*e+tau_ff-Kd*omega-b*omega=Kp*e+tau_ff-(Kd+b)*omega. Kd_sim=Kd_MIT+b is valid deliberately lumped simulation approximation with same velocity coordinate/signal/timing, no duplicated b, ideal torque response; qualifies earlier recommendation, not a denial of algebra. Nonzero target speed adds unwanted b*target speed. Arbitrary little-bit increase is tuning, not measured b.

2026.10.16 Saturation prevents general equivalence even with target speed 0: separate net=clip(X-Kd*omega, +/-limit)-b*omega versus lumped=clip(X-(Kd+b)*omega, +/-limit), X=Kp*e+tau_ff. Fresh synthetic counterexample Kp 10, target/current 2/0 rad, speed 10 rad/s, Kd 0.2, b 0.05, limit 5.6 Nm: separate pre cap 18, actuator 5.6, passive -0.5, net 5.1 Nm; lumped Kd 0.25 pre cap 17.5, actuator/net 5.6 Nm. Both asserted forward-force cases pass, total 6 synthetic cases with API checks. Illustrative values, not measured motor output/balance prediction. Separate passive properties preferable for frequently saturating policy; filtering/sample/current-loop differences also break ideal equivalence. Raising real MIT Kd adds braking on top of natural friction, not merely representing existing b.

2026.10.19 Per-observation sensor feasibility(one-based, not physical certification): 1 gravity Y/2 gravity Z can approximate from calibrated quaternion, but inherit AHRS dynamic error/timing; 3 gyro X/4 gyro Y/5 gyro Z directly available after bias/mount/unit check, 5 is angular RATE not absolute heading available 6/9 mode; 6 vx=0.07*rear_speed is wheel circumference proxy NOT true body vx; 7 vy=0 is NOT measurement. 8 steer A3 rad requires straight startup/reference/gearing/play check; 9 rear A3 cumulative available but wrapped/process zero differs from native Isaac branch/reset. 10 steer A2/11 rear A2 rad/s direct signed speeds, slip does not invalid wheel angvel but breaks frame vx proxy. 12 sinsteer/13 cossteer derive 8, inherit errors/no extra sensor. 2026.10.19 Gravity discussion: standard specific force f_b=R^T*(a_w-g_w)+bias+noise, absolute motion acceleration AT IMU includes lever arm; policy gravity=R^T*[0, 0, -1], not raw specific force. At rest ideal convention g_body~-f_b/g0; device axes/sign/g unit must verify. Fixed static lean legitimately changes components; rapid rotation/lean, accel/braking/circling/vibration contaminate gravity-only inference. Normalization does not remove acceleration. Illustration NOT bike data: upright horizontal circle v 2 m/s, r 1 m, a 4 m/s2, false lean atan(4/9.81)=22.183 deg, norm 1.080 g. Banked turn apparent force can align bike vertical despite true lean.

2026.10.19 Quaternion gravity formula works for any motion IF attitude correct; actual onboard fusion has possible bias/filter delay/dynamic acceleration error, not guaranteed true orientation. Pure heading error alone leaves gravity unchanged, not proof coupled filter errors absent. Actual contracts.py CPU check roll 10/pitch 5 deg, yaw 0/90/179 gives identical float32 gravity[-0.1729873866, -0.9810602665], max difference 0; synthetic invariance NOT AHRS accuracy/mode verification. Do NOT replace raw accel y/z into gravity obs without validated estimator. Proposed static/dynamic external tilt truth+raw accel/gyro/quatfield timing tests NOT performed. References https://rotations.berkeley.edu/strapdown-inertial-navigation/ and https://www.vectornav.com/resources/inertial-navigation-primer/theory-of-operation/theory-ahrs.

2026.10.19 Linear vel actor necessity: stationary balance allows instantaneous micro-motion; true body vx/vy useful for recovery/drift, but NOT proved indispensable for anew policy. Existing 91850 true->proxy 30 s->0.22 s nominal/27.379->0.263 s DR proves frozen policy sensitivity, NOT failure of retraining without channels. Proposed controlled new training: other 11 only versus deployment-consistent velocity estimator versus true velocity baseline, matched plant/reset/seed/DR/deaths. Optional history/recurrence helps hidden motion not guaranteed slip truth; true velocity can stay reward/diagnostic or privileged asymmetric critic(training only), actor IMU/encoders only. Asymmetric reference https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.06542. None of these variants/new training/export implemented; do NOT delete/zero 91850 inputs and assume fix.

2026.10.19 Both angle observations clarification: Isaac reads data.joint_pos directly in radians, NOT encoder counts or q-q_start. bicycle_cfgjoint_pos{".*": 0}/joint_vel{".*": 0}, reset_scene_to_default WRITES default joint state each env reset; it does NOT record first feedback as offset. 2026.10.20 Envelope/peak duration: achievable torque-speed pairs depend voltage/current/temp/mode, NOT stall torque at max speed rectangle. Four-equation steady iq=(vq-Ke*omega)/Rs, tau=Kt*iq plus current ceiling can create flat then voltage limited region; real curve NOT necessarily global line. Kd changes request NOT available torque bounds. Peak duration needs heating/history/cooling/protection; 4 equations lack Tstate. 2026.10.25 Rear graph clarification plus NEW force logging in the comparison report's friction_diagnostic/. Isaac green=get_dof_projected_joint_forces, a solver incoming joint-axis effort; MuJoCo green=data.actuator_force, motor output BEFORE separate joint losses. They are not identical sensors or automatically total wheel-accelerating torque. Both ideal rear controllers submit scaled feedforward with Kp=Kd=0. Original Isaac moving/motoring median |command|-|solver|=0.1059938222 Nm across 158780 samples; command-0.106*sign(speed) matches within 0.0001 Nm on 75.6652% of 494061 ordinary moving samples, not universally. Friction opposes motion; during braking it can increase combined resisting magnitude. Do NOT subtract another 0.106 from MuJoCo's motor command, which would double-count the configured loss.

2026.10.27 Likely numerical cause: native POST-STEP mass matrices give unloaded effective rear J=.00014421-.00021464 kg m^2. Simplified explicit pole 1-dt*Kd/J is -2.467..-1.329 at 400 (64/64 outside unit magnitude), -.734..-.165 at 800 (64/64 inside). Supports overcorrection at this timestep/inertia; friction stick/slip may contribute, not separately ablated. Scalar bound worst Kd<.115366 at 400, or frequency >693.45 Hz with Kd .2; NOT exact multibody proof or real-motor gain/loop limit. Smaller gain may stabilize but increases P tracking offset. Validate smaller timestep/controller integration and grounded DR, retain policy 50 Hz; do not invent inertia/armature. Pi packet rate need not equal simulated internal integration. PRODUCTION STILL 400 Hz/Kd .2; instability not fixed.

2026.10.27 MIT velocity law is PROPORTIONAL speed feedback, despite gain named Kd; no integral, no guaranteed exact tracking. Zero speed error gives zero torque, unable to balance drag. Observed simulated .47 rad/s for target 1 is consistent with .2*(1-.47)=.106 Nm. Supports earlier .53-rad/s simple moving-load estimate, not physical MIT measurement or static-breakaway identification. KEEP dynamic friction .106 Nm, provisional static .106, viscous 0; controller compensation does not remove physical friction. Calibrated feedforward or suitable integral control may remove offset subject to stability/limits. No friction/gain/frequency/controller-type change made in discussion.

2026.10.27 C0/C1 CLARIFICATION (MD 217): user meant C1, not C0. Protocol C0 is Q current .001 A, torque=Kt*current; an inner CURRENT PI does not integrate SPEED error. Same externally calculated P velocity torque through C0 does not automatically remove friction offset/oscillation. C1 takes signed .01 rpm; B8/B9 expose speed Kp/Ki, B3 current ceiling, B5 acceleration .01 rpm/s. With suitable nonzero Ki, stability and torque authority, speed PI can accumulate torque to overcome drag and reduce steady error to zero. NOT guaranteed oscillation cure; no matched C1/MIT experiment or exact internal-loop-rate identification here.

2026.10.28 TORQUE-ACTION RL REVIEW (MD 224): direct torque policies exist on real hardware; distinguish from position policies followed by PD. Chen/A1 uses 200-Hz direct torque for terrain/disturbance rejection and fewer PD gains; reports successful transfer/tracking, not universal superiority (https://arxiv.org/html/2203.05194). Kim/TOCABI real walking uses 62.5/125/250-Hz torque policies for compliance, randomizing current-to-torque constant, friction/damping and delay (https://arxiv.org/html/2304.09434). Allshire/TriFinger uses 50-Hz torques for grasp/lift/reorientation with DR/noise (https://arxiv.org/html/2108.09779). Bargellini/Panda 2026 PREPRINT uses torque RL reaching with identified friction/inertia/compensation and separately measured signed static breakaway (https://arxiv.org/html/2608.22629). Moving drag alone does not establish static threshold.

2026.10.28 Literature discusses uncertain/changing motor dynamics, NOT proof torque control is intrinsically unusable/random. Gurtler real-robot benchmark explicitly describes battery/thermal changes, friction/wear, quantization, delays and hardware variation (https://arxiv.org/html/2307.15690). ANYmal instead uses position actions plus learned actuator response from hardware data (https://arxiv.org/html/1901.08652); position control still requires a faithful actuator model. Practical sequence: identify command response, validate held-out trajectories, calibrate simulation and residual-uncertainty training. Our exact replay is project-specific. A1 fresh-feedback policy 200 Hz is NOT our 50-Hz policy resending 200 Hz; lower-rate TriFinger/TOCABI successes do not establish this bike's needed rate. No justification here to equate Pi CAN with simulated 800/1000-Hz internal integration.

2026.10.28 HUANG BASIS CHECK (MD 225): local isaaclab_train/papers/45°车把转角下前轮驱动自行车机器人的定车运动_黄用华.pdf, DOI 10.3901/JME.2012.07.016, is FRONT-wheel drive, fixed 45-degree bar, partial feedback linearization, NOT RL. P20 section 4.2 uses 33.3-ms measurement/control; desired torque converted to VOLTAGE with u=La*dI/dt+Ra*I+CE*n and torque=N*CT*I; p21 torque calculated from measured current. Supports trackstand mechanism, not our ideal rear/C0 model. Closer rear precedent: Wang EABC-MPC, Pi4B MPC100/feedback200 Hz, direct rear torque and steering velocity PI1000 Hz, with disturbance/equilibrium adaptation (https://arxiv.org/html/2410.19615; local Wang_2024_trackstand_control.pdf). Model-based, not RL, and not proof velocity actions cannot work.

2026.10.28 ENCODER CLARIFICATION (MD 235): A3 contains both little-endian channels: bytes 1-2 unsigned 16-bit SINGLE-turn logged as encoder_single_turn_count and range-checked; bytes 3-6 signed 32-bit MULTI-turn logged as encoder_count. Rear sign -1/gear 1: position_unwrapped_rad=-(multi_count-zero_count)*2pi/16384; breakaway separately references ramp-start multi-count. Wrapped position_rad also logged for display, but summary uses UNWRAPPED displacement. Starting-angle check uses multi-count difference from mark A modulo 360 degrees only for physical orientation; it does not wrap displacement or switch to single-turn measurement.
